# Invariants

What each shipped surface guarantees, in one sentence, and the test that pins it.
Read the matching row before changing a surface; when a change adds or alters a
guarantee, update this register in the same change. Every `theme/` subsystem has a
section, and the render/fidelity tooling lives under [tooling](#tooling) because it
is not a `theme/` subsystem. `tests/support/invariants.py` cross-checks this file
against the repository: a `theme/` subsystem with no section, a section naming
no `theme/` subsystem or extra section, a repeated section heading, a row naming
a test that does not exist, a row naming no test, a malformed table row, or a
blank guarantee or provenance-exception cell fails `make check`.

## aurorae

| Guarantee | Test | Provenance exception |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The active title-bar boxes are re-derived from the reference screenshots; the inactive states ship no art because the reference's unfocused windows (macos8.6-screenshots/desktop_fandom.png) draw buttonless flat bars with no close, zoom or collapse box, and each button SVG's inactive-center group carries one paintless full-canvas path (never a rect) so KSvg's hasElementPrefix("inactive") resolves true: /usr/share/kwin/aurorae/AuroraeButton.qml (KWin 6.3.6) draws the buttonActive overlay's active art on unfocused windows when supportsInactive is false, and an empty group element's zero bounds read as false. | `tests/test_aurorae_buttons.py::TestButtons` | The reference's unfocused windows draw no close, zoom or collapse box in their title bars (macos8.6-screenshots/desktop_fandom.png), so the inactive state has no box art to pin: the paintless path re-derives that drawing, and its geometry is the KWin fallback above. |
| The active frame nine-slice is re-derived from the reference screenshots, the inactive nine-slice from `desktop_fandom.png`'s four unfocused windows -- buttonless flat `#DDDDDD` bars whose centered title text `org.macos8.desktoprc`'s `InactiveTextColor` paints `#777777`, with no separate focus look (Platinum's model is active=frontmost) -- and the gzipped `decoration.svgz` matches the uncompressed artwork. | `tests/test_aurorae_frame.py::TestDecorationSvg` | none |
| Maximized active windows retain the reference-derived 22px pinstriped title bar, maximized inactive windows retain the flat `#DDDDDD` bar, and the Aurorae QML selects those center tiles without side or bottom borders. | `tests/test_aurorae_frame.py::TestDecorationSvg::test_maximized_title_tiles_preserve_active_and_inactive_bars` | The zoomed reference surfaces are the toastytech Mac OS 8.1 gallery screenshots recorded in `BUGS-FIXED.md`; they show the same Platinum title treatment. |
| Maximized title buttons start 4px below the title-bar top edge, matching the reference box placement. | `tests/test_kwin_decoration_package.py::TestMaximizedButtonPlacement::test_maximized_margin_matches_reference_pixels` | The zoomed references recorded in `BUGS-FIXED.md` retain normal title treatment; the lossless `aboutsystem_betawiki.png` supplies the measured inset. |
| The title-bar height, border widths and `TitleEdgeBottom` (the white highlight rows the frame's centre tile keeps between the title bar and the client) that the Aurorae engine reads are re-derived from the reference screenshots. | `tests/test_aurorae_rc.py::TestRc` and `tests/test_render_surface.py::TestRender::test_first_client_rows_match_the_reference` | none |
| The Aurorae package's `metadata.json` / `metadata.desktop` pair is what KPackage and KWin's Aurorae discovery read, `make install` copies the package byte for byte, and `make uninstall` removes it. | `tests/test_aurorae_package.py` | none |

## kwin-decoration

| Guarantee | Test | Provenance exception |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The `KWin/Decoration` KPackage (added 2026-11-04, planned by the plan role) ships unchanged: `make install` copies `theme/kwin-decoration/org.macos8.desktop/` byte for byte into `kwin/decorations/` and repeats without residue, `make uninstall` removes it whole, and the look-and-feel defaults select it -- their `[kwinrc][org.kde.kdecoration2]` pair `library=org.kde.kwin.aurorae` / `theme=org.macos8.desktop` names the package id beside the unchanged aurorae plugin library (KWin 6.3.6 matches this plugin's KPackage under `kwin/decorations/` by `KPlugin.Id`). | `tests/test_kwin_decoration_package.py::TestInstall`, `tests/test_theme_defaults.py` | none |
| The shipped-selection KPackage route loads and draws byte-equal (added 2026-11-04 by the feature role): the singleton loads through the package qmldir's implicit import after the three session-route fixes recorded in its comments (`pragma Singleton`, the explicit `import QtQuick` its `QtObject` root and `color` valuetypes need, and `Qt.resolvedUrl(...).toString()` before the `file://` prefix walk), and in one headless session the calculator render under `theme=org.macos8.desktop` equals the calculator render under the earlier `__aurorae__svg__org.macos8.desktop` SVG selection byte for byte (identical sha-256), while a sabotaged installed `kwin/decorations` QML copy the components need makes KWin draw its frameless fallback and the comparison fail loudly instead of silently passing, under both selections. | `tests/test_kwin_decoration_package.py::TestSingleton` (the static pin of the three fixes) | KWin 6.3.6's `Helper::loadComponent()` selection behavior is session-level: the equality and the sabotage-failure runs are observation evidence recorded here and in docs/rendering.md, each run on this host with the render prerequisites present; the static test pins the singleton spelling that made the route load, not the pixels. |
| The package copies track the engine without drift: `Decoration.qml` is byte-equal to `/usr/share/kwin/aurorae/`'s file, `AuroraeButtonGroup.qml` equals its engine file with only the synchronised token substitution `auroraeTheme.` -> `AuroraeTheme.` (5 spellings), and the hit-surface copies (`DecorationButton.qml`, `MenuButton.qml`, `AuroraeButton.qml`, `AuroraeMaximizeButton.qml` and `AppMenuButton.qml`) equal their engine files with only their dated comment and the window-activity gate (`DecorationButton.qml`, `MenuButton.qml` and `AuroraeMaximizeButton.qml` equal-plus-gate; the 2026-11-05 shadow fix adds the same gate to `AuroraeButton.qml` and the `AppMenuButton.qml` fallback) -- added 2026-11-04 by the feature role and corrected 2026-11-05; an engine update fails instead of silently changing the package's QML. The 2026-11-12 caption gap adds one more equal-plus-addition copy: `main.qml` equals its engine file `aurorae.qml` with the token substitution plus the dated caption-gap `Rectangle` before the caption `Text` (the solid `#CCCCCC` band the width of the painted caption plus 4px each side, spanning the stripe rows, the way Mac OS 8.6 clears the pinstripes behind the title text; reference `apps_macrumors8.6.jpg`, pixel columns sampled 2026-11-12), and the headless calculator render's title-bar strip still byte-matches the reference after the addition. | `tests/test_kwin_decoration_package.py::TestCopies` | No reference screenshot shows a KWin decoration package: the package reuses the existing aurorae art and rc, and upstream KWin 6.3.6's `Helper::loadComponent()` (src/plugins/kdecorations/aurorae/src/aurorae.cpp) is the surface the copies must match. |
| The title-bar hit areas press no invisible button on an unfocused window (added 2026-11-04 by the feature role; corrected 2026-11-05 by the operator): the imported `org.kde.kwin.decoration` module also exports a `DecorationButton`, and the explicit import wins over the package's same-named local file, so the effective gate is the `AuroraeButton.qml` subclass item's `enabled` (false unless `decoration.client.active`, then the base's capability switch) (and the `AppMenuButton.qml` fallback's `enabled: decoration.client.active`), while `MenuButton.qml`'s and `AuroraeMaximizeButton.qml`'s standalone MouseAreas carry `enabled: decoration.client.active` alone; a click where the flat unfocused bar shows no box falls through to the title bar's activation and active windows' boxes stay drawn and clickable; the existing button SVGs' digests pin the art the gating did not touch, keeping the paintless `inactive-center` pins in `tests/test_aurorae_buttons.py` true. | `tests/test_aurorae_ui_qml.py::TestHitSurfaces` | Live click behavior is session-level and unpinnable headlessly, so the pin is the QML gating expressions plus the 2026-11-05 live click that found the module shadow (a synthetic absolute pointer clicked an unfocused close cell; the window activated and stayed open) and the part 2/3 byte-identical render proving art and geometry unchanged; no reference screenshot shows a KWin decoration package (the package reuses the existing aurorae art and rc). |
| The withheld `auroraeTheme` context property has an in-package answer: `contents/ui/qmldir` declares the `AuroraeTheme` singleton, its property set is exactly the set the copies spell, the rc-covered metrics re-derive from `org.macos8.desktoprc` as upstream `Aurorae::ThemeConfig::load` reads them, the border and button metrics spell the values upstream `AuroraeTheme::borders()`/`themeconfig.h` defaults derive, and the `path:` values walk five package levels to the aurorae art `make install` places beside the package, with files the art family does not ship spelled empty as upstream `BUTTON_PATH` does. | `tests/test_kwin_decoration_package.py::TestSingleton` | upstream themeconfig.cpp/themeconfig.h and auroraetheme.cpp are not installed on the target machine; the derivations recorded in the singleton's comments were read from upstream KWin v6.3.6 (the same aurorae engine the SVG path runs), and the 96 dpi derivation values are what a default headless render gate session receives (a non-default system border/button size or dpi scales what other surfaces drive, which the static singleton cannot re-derive -- selection work proves the pixels instead). |
| The package metadata KPackage and the selection code read (added 2026-11-04): `KPackageStructure` `KWin/Decoration`, `KPlugin.Id` `org.macos8.desktop`, Platinum display labels. | `tests/test_kwin_decoration_package.py::TestMetadata` | none |
| The beta `aboutsystem_betawiki.png` window chrome is pixel-identical to the retail `systemprofiler_betawiki.png`. | `tests/test_aurorae_reference_equivalence.py::TestReferenceEquivalence` | `aboutsystem_betawiki.png` is a beta build used as a window-chrome proxy; the render gate's bottom-border crop stays on it because the set has no other lossless window-bottom crop (README, `macos8.6-screenshots/sources.txt`). |

No claim is made about future Plasma updates' consumers, about desktop-theme nodes outside `widgets/`, `dialogs/` and `solid/`, or about any project-owned pixel value — the shipped art's grounding decisions live in the rest of this table. |

## firefox

| Guarantee | Test | Provenance exception |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The standalone `userChrome.css` paints the Platinum palette on Firefox's tab strip, navigation bar, toolbars, address-bar results rows, toolbar button states, address-bar button hover and pressed/open fills, bookmark items, menus, menu separators, find bar (whose unfocused text-box paints a white face with black text and yields its not-found, flash and focus states to Firefox) and status panel with no at-rule and no import. A hovered, unselected tab paints the #EEEEEE trough face, guarded away from selected and multiselected tabs. A tab in a multi-selection (`[multiselected]`) paints the same `#EEEEEE` selected face and open bottom border as the active tab. Menu and arrow-panel body background, text and border use `--panel-background-color`, `--panel-text-color` and `--panel-border-color`, restricted to `menupopup` and `panel[type="arrow"]`, with obsolete menu tokens absent on menus and `--arrowpanel-background`, `--arrowpanel-color` and `--arrowpanel-border-color` absent from parsed rules. Menu and arrow-panel bodies have square corners through a radius token restricted to `menupopup` and `panel[type="arrow"]`. Explicit XUL tooltips have a flat pale-yellow face, black text and 1px outline, square corners, no native appearance and no shadow; native anonymous tooltips and page content are excluded. Results paints target row states and the body separator directly, leaving shared urlbarview tokens for realtime sub-items and one-off buttons to Firefox. Both toolbar fields wear the existing unfocused recipe — white face, square 1px black border, no shadow, no colour declaration — on `#urlbar:not([focused], [open]) > .urlbar-background` and `#searchbar-new:not([focused], [open]) > .urlbar-background`, while each field's focused and open paints and the results bubble's ground stay Firefox's; the classic `#searchbar:not(:focus-within)` rule keeps its white face, black text and square border for a user who disables the default pref. | `tests/test_firefox_theme_shell.py::TestFirefoxChromeShell` `tests/test_firefox_theme_shell.py::TestFirefoxChromeShell::test_toolbar_field_paint_is_scoped_to_the_unfocused_fields` `tests/test_firefox_theme_shell.py::TestFirefoxChromeShell::test_find_field_foreground_is_guarded` `tests/test_firefox_theme_shell.py::TestFirefoxChromeShell::test_tab_hover_paint_is_scoped_to_unselected_tabs` `tests/test_firefox_theme_shell.py::TestFirefoxChromeShell::test_selected_face_covers_multiselected_tabs` | No reference screenshot shows Firefox chrome, so the palette comes from the pinned Platinum values and the geometry is this theme's adaptation of the Platinum controls; Menu-popup zero radius follows the sheet square tabs and address field; arrow-panel zero radius follows those surfaces and square menus, with no arrow-panel reference available (platform geometry adaptations 2026-10-25). Arrow-panel colours reuse the Platinum menu face and black because no reference shows that surface (platform colour adaptation 2026-10-25). Menu and arrow-panel colour tokens follow Firefox toolkit's `chrome/toolkit/skin/classic/global/popup.css` consumer (adapted 2026-10-25); Tooltip palette/geometry adaptation 2026-10-25: no reference shows Firefox tooltips or Balloon Help; yellow uses the accepted classic-palette `[Colors:Tooltip] BackgroundNormal` / `TOOLTIP_FACE` exception in QUESTIONS.md. The square face and black outline follow `widgets/tooltip.svg`, pinned by `tests/test_desktoptheme_tooltip.py::TestTooltip.test_tooltip_pixels`. Toolkit `chrome/toolkit/content/global/xul.css` supplies the `tooltip` selector and native-anonymous limitation. `tests/test_firefox_theme_shell.py::TestFirefoxChromeShell.test_tooltip_face_is_scoped_to_explicit_tooltips` pins yellow scope. Declaration checks do not establish rendered menu, arrow-panel, tooltip, toolbar-field, search-field, find-field, hovered-tab or multi-selected-tab pixels. Unfocused field adaptation 2026-10-25: no reference shows Firefox's optional classic search field; its square 1px black border and shadow-free face follow this sheet's existing field recipe, and black text follows the shared Platinum foreground. Firefox's `chrome/browser/skin/classic/browser/urlbar-searchbar.css` paints `#searchbar` and owns its focused appearance; the field geometry is not screenshot-sampled. Toolbar-field binding adaptation 2026-10-30: the existing `#urlbar-background` selector matched nothing in the installed Firefox ESR 153.6.0esr — `UrlbarInput.mjs`'s `#markup` emits a class-only `<html:div class="urlbar-background"/>` and no element carries `id="urlbar-background"` (probed against the extracted omni.ja 2026-10-30) — and `defaults/preferences/firefox.js`'s default `browser.search.widget.new=true` makes `#searchbar-new` the toolbar search widget, so the rule keys `.urlbar-background` under `#urlbar` and `#searchbar-new` and both toolbar fields now carry the existing unfocused recipe, each field's focused and open paints and the results bubble's ground staying Firefox's; the classic `#searchbar` rule stays live for a user who disables the pref. Hovered-tab adaptation 2026-10-26: no reference shows Firefox tab hover; the face reuses the pinned Platinum trough #EEEEEE from the desktop theme (also the selected tab face) rather than a screenshot sample. Firefox's `chrome/browser/skin/classic/browser/tabbrowser/tabs.css` consumes `--tab-background-color-hover`, which this sheet's greater user-origin `.tab-background` rule suppresses; the trough is pinned by `tests/test_colorscheme_reference.py::TestReferenceAnchors.test_view_background_alternate` and `tests/test_desktoptheme_scrollbar.py::TestScrollbarReference`. Multi-selected-tab adaptation 2026-10-26: no reference shows Firefox multi-selected tabs; the multiselected face reuses the same pinned Platinum trough #EEEEEE as the selected tab rather than a screenshot sample. Firefox's `chrome/browser/skin/classic/browser/tabbrowser/tabs.css` consumes `--tab-background-color-selected` on `.tab-background:is([selected], [multiselected])`, and `chrome/browser/content/browser/tabbrowser/tab.js` sets the tab's `selected` and `multiselected` attributes, mirroring `selected=visuallyselected` onto `.tab-background`; the declaration checks do not establish rendered pixels. Find-field black-text adaptation 2026-10-26: no reference shows Firefox's find field; its white face pairs with the black text of the shared Platinum foreground, pinned by `tests/test_colorscheme_reference.py::TestReferenceAnchors.test_window_chrome_foreground` and `test_window_view_background`, and Firefox toolkit's `chrome/toolkit/skin/classic/global/findbar.css` assigns `.findbar-textbox` its input text token (and, under `:root[lwtheme]`, its toolbar-field text token) while focus, not-found and flash states have separate foregrounds. The guarded text-box shadow follows this sheet's Platinum fields and is not screenshot-sampled. |
| The Firefox install copies both sheets, `userChrome.css` and `userContent.css`, byte-for-byte, each with its own first-line marker, adds only the marker-delimited preference block (the legacy-stylesheet switch and the title-bar preference that hands the window frame to KWin) while preserving foreign `user.js` lines and endings, adopts an empty sheet of either name, refuses a non-empty foreign sheet of either name, and removal reverses only those three changes, recognizing a block written by the earlier single-preference version so its line goes with the marker; a failed install restores the previous sheets and `user.js` (or removes any file it created) whenever a write step raises, including an interrupt delivered after a sheet or `user.js` write has taken effect; a removal rewrites `user.js` first and puts the previous text back, and writes back every owned sheet an earlier unlink already deleted with the text the removal read, whenever the removal raises while any owned sheet remains, so a removal that fails partway leaves the profile exactly as it was, while an interrupt after the last sheet unlink has taken effect keeps the completed rewrite, so a step that fails or is interrupted does not leave the old preference block beside a new sheet; a rollback that itself fails is reported by name alongside the original error instead of hiding the half-applied set; a leading UTF-8 BOM does not hide either sheet marker or our `user.js` marker block, so a BOM-prefixed marker-led sheet of either name is still replaced or removed, a BOM-prefixed `user.js` block is not duplicated by a repeat install and is removed with its preference, and a foreign first line stays untouched in any of the three files; a FIFO at any of the three profile files is refused by name as not a regular file before either sheet is written, instead of blocking the install on an open that waits for a writer, and profile discovery likewise refuses a FIFO at either `installs.ini` or `profiles.ini` by name instead of blocking before any profile file is written, and it tries every `profiles.ini` section that names a `Path` after a stale `Default=1` marker, listing each candidate once in its not-found diagnostic; an empty `FIREFOX_PROFILE` is treated as unset, so the targets fall back to discovery as they do when it is absent, matching the Makefile's empty `$(if ...)` value; `--optional`, which `make apply` passes, returns success with a note instead of an error when discovery finds no existing profile, while a named missing or relative profile still fails. A profile file larger than the installer's 1 MiB read cap is refused by name after one byte past the cap instead of being read whole, and profile discovery applies the same 1 MiB cap to `installs.ini` and `profiles.ini`, so an oversize INI is refused by name instead of being read whole by configparser; and the install refuses by name, before writing any file, a `user.js` whose marker block would grow it past that same cap, so a profile the installer creates can always be read back and removed; a sheet or `user.js` path that is a symbolic link is refused by name before the install writes or the removal deletes anything, so the user's link and the file it names are left untouched instead of the link being replaced or deleted; the refusal fires at a path the action plans to change — the install refuses every link at those paths because its write would replace it, while the removal refuses the `chrome` directory up front, then an owned sheet's link and a `user.js` link whose preference block it would rewrite — so a dangling link at a sheet or `user.js` path reads back as an absent file and a removal exits 0 leaving that link in place while it still removes what it owns beside it; an oversize sheet or `user.js` therefore cannot exhaust memory, strand the install behind a file the reader refuses, or escape as a `MemoryError` traceback instead of the documented exit-2 diagnostic; an install or a removal takes an exclusive lock over the profile directory for the whole read-edit-write, so concurrent runs cannot leave a preference block without its sheets or the sheets without the block, the lock adds no lock file to the profile and is released when the process dies, including SIGKILL, and a run that cannot open the profile directory, cannot take the lock, or waits past `LOCK_TIMEOUT` exits 2 with a diagnostic naming the profile and changes nothing. | `tests/test_firefox_user_js.py::TestUserJs`, `tests/test_install_firefox.py::TestInstall`, The mid-list `#nav-separator` is document light DOM — `moz-page-nav.mjs` slots it and styles `hr` only in its own shadow tree, whose rules cannot match slotted content — so the content sheet paints the full etched pair on it directly. The shadow `hr` and the `:host` inline-end border each take one colour through one custom property (`--page-nav-hr-background-color` and `--page-nav-border-color`, read 2026-10-27 from `chrome/toolkit/content/global/elements/moz-page-nav.css` and `moz-page-nav.tokens.css`), so a two-tone etch is impossible there; the flat `#888888` dark half is this theme's own choice, with the border width and the rule's margins left at Firefox's. In the installed Firefox ESR 153.5.1, `chrome/browser/content/browser/preferences/preferences.xhtml` builds the sidebar as `moz-page-nav#categories` of `moz-page-nav-button` hosts whose `chrome/toolkit/content/global/elements/moz-page-nav-button.css` paints the inner `a[href]`/`button` from the `:host` tokens with no `::part()` exposed, so the content sheet overrides the tokens on the host element. A headless run of this ESR on 2026-10-27 confirmed the URL match, the sheet's reach and that outer-sheet custom properties override the shadow `:host` defaults, so the token route reuses that verification. The pane follows: Firefox paints the page from `:root`'s `background-color-canvas` in `chrome/toolkit/skin/classic/global/in-content/common-shared.css` and each heading band — the `hbox.subcategory` rows of `#mainPrefPane` holding each pane's `html:h1` and the search-results `html:h2` — from `.subcategory`'s `border-top` in `chrome/browser/skin/classic/browser/preferences/preferences.css` (both read 2026-10-27); the page takes the reference-grounded white view background (`tests/test_colorscheme_reference.py::TestReferenceAnchors.test_window_view_background`) and each band the part 2 etch with black text and an inset white half over the band's own padding — Firefox gives `.subcategory` no bottom border or box-shadow, so the inset shadow overlays only the band's existing top padding and changes no geometry (this theme's own choice); the reach reuses part 1's headless run of 2026-10-27, which confirmed the `@-moz-document url("about:preferences")` block reaches the document light DOM where the `body`, `.main-content`, `#mainPrefPane` and `.subcategory` rows live; the panes' other controls stay Firefox's for later parts (the enabled check boxes are the checkbox row below). Declaration checks do not establish rendered row, separator or pane pixels (platform colour adaptation 2026-10-27). |

The rows include the `.panel-subview-footer-button` rows `#PanelUI-historyMore` and `#panelMenu_showAllBookmarks`, which sit directly in a panelview outside the view's `.panel-subview-body`; the `.panel-header` back and edit buttons also carry `.subviewbutton`, so the sheet excludes every `.panel-header` descendant that carries `.subviewbutton` and leaves those header buttons to Firefox. | `tests/test_firefox_theme_panels.py::TestFirefoxPanelRows::test_app_menu_row_paint_is_scoped_to_the_app_menu` | No reference screenshot shows Firefox's app menu; its row highlight adapts the reference-grounded `widgets/viewitem.svg` and the sheet's menu-item mapping, pinned by `tests/test_desktoptheme_viewitem.py::TestViewItem` and `tests/test_colorscheme_reference.py::TestReferenceAnchors.test_selection_background` (platform colour adaptation 2026-10-26). The toolkit probe of 2026-10-30 (`unzip -p /usr/lib/firefox-esr/omni.ja chrome/toolkit/res/scrollbars.css`, showing only `-moz-default-appearance:` paint on the scrollbar parts) makes the standard `scrollbar-color` (CSS Scrollbars Styling Level 1, Firefox 64+) the mechanism, and its two-face limit means the thumb bevel, the outlines and the steppers are out of its reach. GTK shared stylesheet parser (recorded 2026-11-01 by the feature role):

| Guarantee | Test | Provenance exception |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Named custom properties (a `--` prefix followed by at least one character) may have an empty normalized value, which participates in the same later-wins and `!important` cascade as other declarations; empty ordinary values and bare `--` remain malformed. | `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_empty_custom_property_values_preserve_grouping_and_priority`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_empty_custom_property_at_semicolon_and_block_end`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_malformed_empty_declarations_keep_source_prefix`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_empty_custom_property_values_are_retained_and_cascade`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_empty_bare_custom_and_ordinary_values_remain_malformed` | none (implemented 2026-11-01) |
| Comment delimiters inside unquoted `url(...)` tokens remain literal through the first unescaped closing parenthesis, including ASCII case variants and optional CSS whitespace; ordinary comments and comment-like text in non-URL functions are removed. | `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestStripComments::test_comment_markers_inside_unquoted_urls_are_text`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_unquoted_url_comment_markers_preserve_following_declarations`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestHostileStylesheet::test_comment_scanning_finishes_promptly` | none (implemented 2026-11-01) |
| Ordinary declaration names decode simple and one-to-six-digit hexadecimal CSS escapes before ASCII case folding and priority-aware merging; custom names decode those escapes before priority-aware merging without case folding. Optional hex terminators (including CRLF) are consumed, invalid scalars become U+FFFD, and a seventh hex digit remains literal. Names with incomplete or newline escapes keep their normalized source spelling; non-ASCII decoded characters retain case, while selectors and values are unchanged. | `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_ordinary_declaration_names_decode_css_escapes_before_merging`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_ordinary_declaration_names_fold_ascii_case`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_escaped_ordinary_declaration_names_are_canonicalized`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_escaped_property_names_share_cascade_priority`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_custom_names_decode_and_invalid_escaped_names_keep_source_spelling`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_custom_property_names_decode_without_case_folding_before_merging`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_custom_property_names_and_non_ascii_name_characters_are_preserved` | none (implemented 2026-11-01) |
| Declaration properties split at the first unescaped colon before declaration-name escape decoding; an escaped colon can therefore decode within an ordinary or custom key without becoming the separator, and missing separators follow the malformed-declaration diagnostic. | `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_escaped_colons_in_declaration_names`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_escaped_colon_without_separator_is_malformed`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_escaped_colon_in_ordinary_name_decodes_without_moving_separator`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_ordinary_declaration_names_decode_css_escapes_before_merging` | none (implemented 2026-11-01) |
| For an exact selector/property pair, an important declaration wins over a normal declaration regardless of order; declarations with equal priority use the later value. The keyword uses ASCII case folding after decoding CSS simple and hexadecimal escapes, and only CSS whitespace (space, tab, LF, CR and form feed) separates the `!` marker from the keyword; invalid escapes, extra identifier characters and other Unicode spacing do not grant priority. The returned value keeps its normalized source spelling, and each selector occurrence remains in the selector list. | `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_important_declarations_win_with_later_ties`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_important_priority_requires_css_whitespace`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_important_keyword_uses_ascii_case_folding`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_important_keyword_decodes_css_escapes`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_important_scanner_ignores_strings_urls_and_escaped_bangs` | none (implemented 2026-11-01) |
| Declaration boundaries trim only CSS whitespace, preserving non-CSS whitespace characters and escaped trailing CSS whitespace in values, while trimming unescaped trailing whitespace and rejecting a non-CSS-whitespace-only malformed chunk. | `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_non_css_whitespace_at_declaration_boundaries_is_preserved`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_escaped_trailing_value_whitespace_is_preserved` | none (implemented 2026-11-01) |
| CSS hex escapes retain their optional whitespace terminator separately from following normalized whitespace in selectors and unquoted values; one to six ASCII hex digits form the escape, CRLF is one terminator, and later digits remain literal text. | `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_hex_escape_selector_terminator_is_distinct_from_separator`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_hex_escape_lengths_and_terminators_in_values`, `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_hex_escape_terminators_survive_selector_and_value_normalization` | none (implemented 2026-11-01) |
| Declaration semicolons split only outside quoted strings, parentheses and square brackets; escaped or quoted brackets do not change bracket depth, nested brackets retain internal semicolons, and stray closing brackets do not hide a following declaration. | `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_semicolons_inside_square_bracket_values_are_preserved` | none (implemented 2026-11-01) |
| Rule braces inside balanced square-bracket values remain literal, including nested and function-grouped tokens; quoted or escaped brackets do not affect depth, and an unmatched closing bracket cannot hide a later rule. Parentheses and square brackets both shield braces from block extraction. | `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_braces_inside_square_bracket_values_are_preserved` | none (implemented 2026-11-01) |

| The standalone GTK 3 Platinum theme redefines the Platinum palette and paints the scoped Platinum control rules, including every plain and reorderable tab state, the progress-bar and scale track, fill and thumb rules, the switch track and thumb, the raised check box and round radio faces (boxless in menus) with their shipped checked glyphs, the combo box dropdown arrow and the menu scroll arrows from their shipped glyphs, the tree-view and list-box row hover and selection rules, the scroll-bar stepper 16px arrow boxes with their `#555555` trough edge painted from the shipped up and down triangles on a normal scroll bar (with the toolkit's own end padding zeroed) and hidden on an overlay one, the title-bar close, maximize and minimize boxes painted from their shipped Aurorae-derived assets, the 22px headerbar floor with its 4px title edge and flat inactive backdrop, and the tooltip, menu-separator and frame rules, without importing Adwaita; | The shared GTK stylesheet parser removes comments and pairs rule blocks without the quadratic backtracking of the regular expressions it replaced, so a contributor-supplied `gtk.css` with many unterminated comment starts or a long unbraced run cannot stall the suite. It splits a selector group at commas outside parentheses, square brackets and quoted strings, so a comma inside an attribute selector's value (`[title="a,b"]`) stays part of that selector and a comma inside `:not(.x, .y)` does not start a new one. It collapses CSS whitespace (space, tab, line feed, carriage return and form feed) between tokens without rewriting non-ASCII spacing characters or quoted strings, so distinct selectors containing U+00A0 or U+2003 remain distinct and an unquoted value keeps those characters; two selectors that differ only inside a quoted value (`[title="a  b"]` and `[title="a b"]`) stay separate rules and a declaration value such as `content: "a  b"` keeps its spaces. A quoted string is literal to the comment, block and `@define-color` scanners too, so a comment marker, a brace or a colour definition inside one is text rather than a delimiter or a definition (a declaration value keeps it and the rule keeps its declarations), and a backslash-escaped quote does not end the string early. Parentheses group values in the block scanner too, so a brace inside any `(...)` (an unquoted `url()` token's braces are CSS url code points) is text rather than a block delimiter, while an unmatched `)` is not tracked, so it cannot hide a later block delimiter. | `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestHostileStylesheet` `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_a_comma_inside_an_attribute_selector_does_not_split_the_group` `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_whitespace_inside_a_quoted_selector_value_is_preserved` `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_whitespace_inside_a_quoted_declaration_value_is_preserved` `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_only_css_ascii_whitespace_collapses_between_tokens` `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_a_brace_inside_a_quoted_string_does_not_pair_a_block` `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_a_brace_inside_an_unquoted_url_value_is_text` `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_a_stray_closing_parenthesis_does_not_hide_a_brace_either` `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestStripComments::test_a_comment_marker_inside_a_quoted_string_is_text` `tests/test_gtk_css.py::TestRuleBlockSemantics::test_a_define_color_inside_a_quoted_value_is_text` | none |
| The GTK install copies the thirteen GTK theme files byte-for-byte, is repeatable, leaves previous files intact when a staging step fails, while a rename that fails during the commit step is reported and is not rolled back after earlier renames, and every install copies them, not only the GTK-only target; `make uninstall` removes the thirteen files, their `.staging` siblings, the emptied `assets`/`gtk-3.0`/`gtk-4.0` directories and then, with `rmdir` on the same run, the emptied `Kvantum/` directory and the emptied shared theme directory `themes/org.macos8.desktop` itself, while a directory still holding a foreign file the theme did not install survives, as does the `themes/` root; and because a published GTK selection left behind names sheets that no longer exist, an uninstall warns (editing nothing) when `[Settings]` of either GTK settings file still holds a `gtk-theme-name=org.macos8.desktop` line or the user's `gtk-4.0/gtk.css` still carries the selection marker comment, reading the ini files the way `tools.gtk_settings.py` reads them and naming `make remove-gtk` as the reset; that hold latches on any `[Settings]` line, as the recipe's original awk's never-unset `found=1` did: only a line in `[Settings]` marks the file, and a later duplicate key naming a foreign theme or a later section switch never unmarks an earlier one of ours, while the ini lookup rule the module's other diagnostics share stays last-value-wins. | `tests/test_gtk_install.py::TestInstall`, `tests/test_gtk_install.py::TestUninstall`, `tests/test_kvantum_install.py::TestInstall::test_make_uninstall_removes_the_installed_files`, `tests/test_gtk_install.py::TestUninstallWarnsForTheDanglingGtkSelection`, `tests/test_gtk_uninstall_diagnostic.py` | No reference screenshot shows a GTK widget; the directory layout is GTK-required, the seven glyph assets are this theme's own, and the three title-button boxes copy the reference-grounded Aurorae widgets. |
| The GTK selection published by `make apply` in `[Settings]` of both GTK settings files preserves other foreign settings content, and the marker `gtk-4.0/gtk.css` that imports the installed GTK 4 sheet replaces a foreign user stylesheet only after copying it byte-for-byte to an unused `.bak` sibling (a pre-existing backup is never overwritten, so `gtk.css.bak.1` and up are used); an existing but empty `gtk.css` is adopted rather than treated as foreign, because it has no content to preserve, and needs no backup. An apply and removal refuse a symlinked `gtk-3.0/` or `gtk-4.0/` directory before reading or changing files, keeping filesystem access beneath the selected config home. A symlinked config home remains supported. Removal needs only the config home. An apply checks both installed sheets before creating or locking any directory. Apply and removal serialize on an exclusive lock over the directory containing the config home; apply also holds the data-home lock through its sheet re-check and selection. | `tests/test_apply_gtk_errors.py::TestSettingsErrors::test_apply_and_remove_refuse_symlinked_config_directories`, `tests/test_apply_gtk_errors.py::TestSettingsErrors::test_a_symlinked_config_home_is_still_supported`, `tests/test_apply_gtk.py`, `tests/test_gtk_user_files.py::TestUserCssBackup`, `tests/test_apply_gtk_locks.py::TestConfigLock`, `tests/test_apply_gtk_locks.py::TestDataHomeLock` | No reference screenshot shows a GTK widget; the settings keys, the config path and the libadwaita user-CSS path are GTK-required. |
| `tools/gtk_settings.py` edits only the `gtk-theme-name` line and keeps every other line's own ending, so a CRLF, lone-CR or mixed-ending `settings.ini` keeps its line endings through `apply_gtk` and `remove-gtk`; the exceptions are that inserting after an unterminated header or appending a section must terminate that line first, and a file without a final line ending still has none after removal. The value written to that line cannot contain a recognized line separator. | `tests/test_gtk_settings.py::TestLineEndings`, `tests/test_apply_gtk.py::TestLineEndings`, `tests/test_gtk_settings.py::TestRejectLineSeparators` | none |
| A GTK settings or user-CSS file that cannot be read or written is reported as one `apply_gtk: error:` line naming the path with exit status 2 instead of a traceback escaping `main`; every settings and user-CSS file is read and every write is staged before any is committed, so a file that cannot be read or staged leaves all of them unchanged; the commit replaces the settings and deletes the user CSS together, so a rename or removal that fails during it is reported by name after every file already changed is restored from its same-directory hard-link backup, including the user CSS, a failed restoration is reported too, a target that was not changed keeps no backup sibling, and a filesystem that refuses the backup link rolls the commit back; each backup gets a unique name, so a pre-existing file at a `.backup` sibling name is left untouched; failure to remove a backup after publishing is reported by name and leaves the published settings in place with the backup available for recovery; a failed user-CSS removal leaves the settings unchanged unless restoring an earlier target also fails, an interrupt raised as an exception during the commit (Ctrl-C's `KeyboardInterrupt`) is rolled back rather than left half-applied, while a signal that ends the process mid-commit is not rolled back; a FIFO at a settings or user-CSS path is refused by name as not a regular file before anything is written or removed rather than being read or replaced, checking the opened descriptor so a regular file swapped for a FIFO before the open cannot block the read; a foreign user CSS is copied to its backup after every write is staged and before the commit, and a copy that fails discards the staging files so no settings file changes; a settings or user-CSS path that is a symbolic link is refused by name before staging any write or removal, so the user's link and the file it names are left untouched instead of the link being replaced or deleted. A symlinked parent directory (a symlinked config home) is not a link at the config path, so it stays supported. A settings or user-CSS file larger than the tool's 1 MiB read cap is refused by name after one byte past the cap instead of being read whole, so an oversize `settings.ini` or `gtk.css` cannot escape as a `MemoryError` traceback; an apply also refuses by name, before staging any write, a settings file whose longer value would grow it past that same cap, so a file this tool writes can always be read back. | `tests/test_apply_gtk_errors.py::TestSettingsErrors`, `tests/test_apply_gtk.py::TestRemove`, `tests/test_apply_gtk_errors.py::TestUnreadableConfig`, `tests/test_apply_gtk_errors.py::TestUnreadableConfig::test_apply_refuses_an_oversize_settings_file_before_writing_anything`, `tests/test_apply_gtk_errors.py::TestUnreadableConfig::test_remove_refuses_an_oversize_user_css_before_clearing_anything`, `tests/test_apply_gtk_errors.py::TestUnreadableConfig::test_a_settings_file_at_the_1_mib_cap_is_accepted`, `tests/test_apply_gtk_errors.py::TestUnreadableConfig::test_an_apply_that_would_grow_a_settings_file_past_the_cap_is_refused`, `tests/test_apply_gtk_errors.py::TestSettingsErrors::test_apply_refuses_a_symlinked_settings_file_before_writing_anything`, `tests/test_apply_gtk_errors.py::TestSettingsErrors::test_a_symlinked_config_home_is_still_supported`, `tests/test_apply_gtk_errors.py::TestSettingsErrors::test_remove_refuses_a_symlinked_settings_file_before_clearing_anything`, `tests/test_apply_gtk_errors.py::TestSettingsErrors::test_remove_refuses_a_symlinked_user_css_before_clearing_anything`, `tests/test_gtk_staging.py::TestCommit`, `tests/test_gtk_staging.py::TestCommit::test_a_backup_cleanup_failure_is_reported_after_publish` | none |

The doubled-seam measurement is from this host on 2026-11-11 with a GTK 3 offscreen child rendering a `Gtk.SpinButton` in a 5px `Gtk.Box`, and the pixel probe pins the rendered columns and stepper faces. |
| GTK 3 horizontal spin fields mirror the existing etch in right-to-left text direction: `spinbutton:dir(rtl):not(.vertical)` declares `box-shadow: inset -1px 1px #999999` directly after the `entry, spinbutton:not(.vertical)` rule, so the RTL field's vertical etch arm sits at its exposed right edge — the corner where the LTR field's arm sits — while the base rule's declarations are unchanged. | `tests/test_gtk_theme.py::TestGtkTheme::test_rtl_spin_field_mirrors_the_etch`, `tests/test_gtk_theme_controls.py::TestGtkThemeControls::test_horizontal_spinbutton_steppers_render_one_pixel_seams` | No collected reference screenshot shows a GTK spin button in any direction — the missing reference surface — so the etch is the existing field recipe and the mirror is this theme's adaptation: GTK 3 does not mirror `box-shadow` for text direction, and measured 2026-11-11 on this host with a GTK 3 offscreen child, the shipped sheet left a horizontal RTL spin field with no vertical arm at either exposed edge (the steppers sit left of the field and cover the arm at the left edge, the exposed right edge reading `#999999` at the top arm only, then white). With the mirror the RTL exposed edge reads `#999999` down the arm at rows T+1, T+2, T+3 and B−1 and the pixel probe pins both directions' edge columns. Exclusions with reasons: plain `entry` fields are untouched (only the spin field's overlay is the defect); the four `.osd spinbutton:not(.vertical)` state groups restate the etch at equal-or-higher specificity, so their RTL fields keep the covered arm, and twins for them would multiply the overlay-scoped OSD record across its four states — the OSD family is a future candidate; and the `:not(.vertical)` arm keeps vertical spin fields out of the mirror. Declaration, count, source-order and rendered-edge-column checks; GTK 4 is out of scope (a different spin-button node tree, and its probe cannot run on a host without Xvfb). Recorded 2026-11-11 by the feature role. |
| GTK 3 OSD spin fields mirror the existing etch in right-to-left text direction in all four OSD states: the four twins `.osd spinbutton:dir(rtl):not(.vertical)`, `.osd spinbutton:dir(rtl):focus:not(.vertical)`, `.osd spinbutton:dir(rtl):backdrop:not(.vertical)` and `.osd spinbutton:dir(rtl):disabled:not(.vertical)` each declare exactly `box-shadow: inset -1px 1px #999999` directly after their base state group in the OSD entry-field record, so the RTL OSD field's vertical etch arm sits at its exposed right edge — each twin out-ranks its base group by one class, so the win does not depend on source position, and the base groups' declarations, the grouped `.osd entry` arms and the existing plain mirror rule are unchanged. | `tests/test_gtk_theme_osd.py::TestGtkThemeOsd::test_osd_entry_fields`, `tests/test_gtk_theme_osd.py::TestGtkThemeOsd::test_osd_spin_field_mirrors_the_etch_in_rtl`, `tests/test_gtk_theme_controls.py::TestGtkThemeControls::test_osd_spin_field_renders_the_mirrored_etch_in_rtl` | No collected reference screenshot shows a field inside a GTK OSD overlay — the missing reference surface, the standing premise the OSD entry-field record's comment keeps — so the etch is the existing field recipe and the mirror is this theme's adaptation: GTK 3 does not mirror `box-shadow` for text direction, and the four base groups restated the etch at equal-or-higher specificity than the existing plain mirror, so it never reached an OSD RTL field — measured 2026-11-11 on this host with a GTK 3 offscreen child, the shipped sheet read `#999999`, `#FFFFFF`, `#FFFFFF`, `#FFFFFF` at the RTL field's exposed right edge. With the twins the rendered OSD spin field reads `#999999` down the arm at the exposed edge column (rows T+1, T+2, T+3 and B−1) in both directions — LTR `L+1` grey and `R−1` black, RTL `L+1` black and `R−1` grey — and LTR is otherwise unchanged. Plain `entry` fields stay untouched — they have no steppers to cover the arm, so the existing plain-mirror exclusion's reason stands for them. This resolves the existing plain-mirror entry's recorded OSD-family exclusion (the future-candidate note beside the `spinbutton:dir(rtl)` rule). The rendered edge-column probe pins the base state's pixels: an offscreen window cannot take focus and GTK cannot force the backdrop state in this probe, so the focused, backdrop and disabled twins' rendered reach follows from the same one-class-higher cascade restatement but their pixels stay covered by the declaration, count and source-position checks — a twin dropped, misspelled or moved away from its base group fails those tests, and a dropped or misspelled base twin fails the pixel probe. GTK 4 is out of scope (a different spin-button node tree). Recorded 2026-11-11 by the feature role. |

The measured render is from this host on 2026-11-11 with a GTK 3 offscreen child rendering a vertical `Gtk.SpinButton` in a 5px `Gtk.Box`: exactly four fully-black rows — top edge, the two field/stepper joins, bottom edge — no adjacent pair, in LTR and RTL, with the raised up-stepper head, the etched field face and the down-stepper tail unchanged. | The GTK 4 CSD window shell — the normal, popup, message-dialog, maximized, fullscreen, tiled variants and the ssd, solid-csd and tooltip top levels — draws square corners with no soft stock shadow. The compositor-less `window.solid-csd` arms paint the hard outline as their own 1px window border (`padding: 0`, stock's bevel cleared, `border-color: #555555` when backdrop and no restated radius or margin, since stock already zeroes them); `window.csd:backdrop` clears stock's 200ms box-shadow transition so focus changes are immediate. | `tests/test_gtk4_theme.py::TestGtk4Theme::test_csd_window_shell`, `tests/test_gtk4_theme.py::TestGtk4Theme::test_csd_window_shell_state_cascade` | No collected reference screenshot shows a GTK window shell, so the shell values are a platform adaptation, not sampled pixels: the corner-zero and outline re-derive the shipped square Aurorae chrome, whose active `#000000` outer outline and inactive `#555555` recolour are pinned by `tests/test_aurorae_frame.py` (the `#555555` values sampled in the unfocused window's outer edge). Declaration and source-order checks do not establish rendered pixels. |

The render is captured by the shared `GTK4_PROBE_PAINTED` helper — the four allocation-pinned probe suites' and the two view-census face children's capture, held in `tests/support/gtk4_child_probe.py` and interpolated into each child program (moved there 2026-11-12 by the dedupe role; the census face children joined 2026-11-12). | A GTK 4 plain push button paints the existing raised recipe: from a resting and a pressed childless `Gtk.Button` (the pressed one carrying the `ACTIVE` state flag before the window presents), each rendered through `Gtk.WidgetPaintable` at the button's own allocation — the allocation is its painted border box 1:1 — the probe pins the `#DDDDDD` centre, `#000000` top border and inset `#FFFFFF`/`#AAAAAA` bevel arms, swapped on the pressed button. | `tests/test_gtk4_button_probe.py::TestGtk4ButtonProbe::test_button_faces_render` | No collected reference screenshot in `macos8.6-screenshots/` shows a GTK push button; values come from the existing button rules and palette constants in `tests/support/platinum_palette.py`. The opt-in probe requires GTK 4 introspection and an X display, and retains its rendered pixel assertions and exit-77 capability skip. The render is captured by the shared `GTK4_PROBE_PAINTED` helper — the four allocation-pinned probe suites' and the two view-census face children's capture, held in `tests/support/gtk4_child_probe.py` and interpolated into each child program (moved there 2026-11-12 by the dedupe role; the census face children joined 2026-11-12). (moved 2026-11-11 by the feature role) |
| A GTK 4 plain slider paints the existing meter recipe: from a resting and an insensitive-parent `Gtk.Scale` (adjustment 0..100 at 50, value drawn off), each rendered through `Gtk.WidgetPaintable` at the scale's own allocation, the probe pins the thumb's interior centre as `#DDDDDD`, its top border row as `#000000` and its bevel arms one pixel inside the borders (`#FFFFFF` top, `#999999` bottom), the trough band `#EEEEEE` on both sides of the thumb (the existing fill and highlight are transparent, so a stock accent fill or highlight leaking through fails either side), the trough's top border row as `#000000` and its top interior row as the `#999999` inset-shadow arm; the disabled scale repeats the trough pins and pins the disabled thumb's flat twin — the top border row `#999999`, not `#000000`, and the former bevel-arm rows `#DDDDDD`, no bevel. | `tests/test_gtk4_scale_probe.py::TestGtk4ScaleProbe::test_scale_faces_render` | No collected reference screenshot in `macos8.6-screenshots/` shows a GTK slider (the standing GTK-scope adaptation premise); values come from the sheet's existing `scale > trough`, `scale > trough > fill, scale > trough > highlight`, `scale > trough > slider` and `scale > trough > slider:disabled` groups (with `scale:disabled > trough > slider`'s second flattening arm) and `BLACK`, `FACE`, `GREY`, `WHITE` and `TROUGH` in `tests/support/platinum_palette.py`, formatted into the child's literal expectations from the suite's own imports so a palette change cannot desynchronize them. The render is captured by the shared `GTK4_PROBE_PAINTED` helper — the four allocation-pinned probe suites' and the two view-census face children's capture, held in `tests/support/gtk4_child_probe.py` and interpolated into each child program (moved there 2026-11-12 by the dedupe role; the census face children joined 2026-11-12). Positions derive from the fresh allocations: the scale's content origin is the sheet's bare `scale` group's 12px padding, child allocations read relative to the parent's content-box origin, and the thumb's allocation is its margin box — the existing `-5px` thumb margin shrinks it — so its painted border box sits 5px outside it and measures 10px more each way, overhanging the band. The opt-in child probe skips unless requested and requires GTK 4 introspection plus a display. Measured 2026-11-11 on this host by running the probe's child program under an isolated headless Wayland session against the shipped sheet: every pin settled at its literal tuple, and sheet mutations — deleting the trough group's inset-shadow arm, repainting the thumb border `#555555`, repainting the fill/highlight group `#CCCCFF` (stock's accent ink) and removing both disabled-thumb flattening arms — each failed the probe on the corresponding pin; the last mutation needed both `scale > trough > slider:disabled` and `scale:disabled > trough > slider` stripped, since the first's removal alone still leaves the second holding `box-shadow: none`. The pressed state stays out: the existing `scale > trough > slider:hover, scale > trough > slider:active` group repaints the same face, border and raised bevel as the resting thumb, so pressed pins would be byte-identical; the spelling stays held by the scale declaration tests. One exposure, shared with the existing child-interpreter probes: the child inherits the running environment, so a host that applies the theme's own user CSS restates the same recipe under the probe, and the measurement ran with a clean scratch `HOME` so only the probe's provider painted. Declaration checks do not establish rendered pixels. (moved 2026-11-11 by the feature role) |
| A plain vertical GTK 4 scrollbar paints the existing trough band, rim and raised thumb: from a resting and an insensitive-parent `Gtk.Scrollbar` (adjustment 0..100 with page 50, the value moved to 25 so the thumb rides off the trough's top border and an interior band exists on both sides), each rendered through `Gtk.WidgetPaintable` at the scrollbar's own allocation, the probe pins the thumb's interior centre as `#DDDDDD`, its top border row as `#000000` and its bevel arms one pixel inside the borders (`#FFFFFF` top, `#999999` bottom), the trough band `#EEEEEE` on the middle rows above and below the thumb, and the trough's left border column as `#000000`; the disabled scrollbar repeats the band, rim and thumb-face pins — the existing `scrollbar > range > trough > slider:disabled` group keeps `opacity: 1`, so the disabled thumb's interior still reads opaque `#DDDDDD` and any state fade fails. | `tests/test_gtk4_scrollbar_probe.py::TestGtk4ScrollbarProbe::test_scrollbar_faces_render` | No collected reference screenshot in `macos8.6-screenshots/` shows a GTK scrollbar (the standing GTK-scope adaptation premise); values come from the sheet's existing `scrollbar > range > trough` and `scrollbar > range > trough > slider` groups and `BLACK`, `FACE`, `GREY`, `WHITE` and `TROUGH` in `tests/support/platinum_palette.py`, formatted into the child's literal expectations from the suite's own imports so a palette change cannot desynchronize them. Positions derive from the fresh allocations in the existing child frame: child allocations read relative to the parent's content-box origin, the trough's painted border box is the range's plus the trough's allocation, and the existing thumb margin is 0, so the slider node's allocation paints 1:1 inside the trough's content box; the child asserts the `scrollbar`/`range`/`trough`/`slider` node names and that a standalone scrollbar carries no `overlay-indicator` style class, so the plain recipe is what the probe pins. The opt-in child probe skips unless requested and requires GTK 4 introspection plus a display. Measured 2026-11-11 on this host by running the probe's child program under an isolated headless Wayland session against the shipped sheet: two clean runs settled with every pin at its literal tuple, and sheet mutations — repainting the trough face `#FF00FF`, repainting the trough border and the thumb border `#555555`, repainting the thumb face `#FF00FF`, replacing the thumb's raised bevel with `box-shadow: none`, and dropping the disabled group's `opacity: 1` to `0.4` — each failed the probe on the corresponding pin. The pressed and fine-tune states stay out: the existing `slider:hover, slider:active` and `range.fine-tune` groups repaint the same face and raised bevel as the resting thumb, so those pins would be byte-identical; the declaration tests hold the spellings. Overlay scrollbars stay out: the probe builds no scrolled window and no overlay-indicator widget, the existing overlay groups restate the same faces, and the overlay declaration tests hold the margins, breadths and outline. One exposure, shared with the existing child-interpreter probes: the child inherits the running environment, so a host that applies the theme's own user CSS restates the same recipe under the probe, and the measurement ran with a clean scratch `HOME` so only the probe's provider painted. The render is captured by the shared `GTK4_PROBE_PAINTED` helper — the four allocation-pinned probe suites' and the two view-census face children's capture, held in `tests/support/gtk4_child_probe.py` and interpolated into each child program (moved there 2026-11-12 by the dedupe role; the census face children joined 2026-11-12). Declaration checks do not establish rendered pixels. (added 2026-11-11 by the feature role) |

| The cursor selection exposes the installed icon theme at `$HOME/.icons/org.macos8.desktop` and selects it with `plasma-apply-cursortheme org.macos8.desktop`; a missing installed theme, `index.theme` or `cursors/left_ptr` is refused, a symlinked `$HOME/.icons` directory is refused before creating a link outside HOME, a foreign path is left untouched, readlink failures are reported as cursor errors naming the path, and removal unlinks only our symlink (including a dangling one), leaving `~/.icons` and `kcminputrc` in place. Removal reports data-home inspection errors and retains the link rather than silently skipping the data-home lock. `--optional`, which `make apply` passes, skips only the selector step with a note when `plasma-apply-cursortheme` is absent, after installing and linking the theme. A SIGTERM while the selector runs kills the selector's process group and removes the scratch directory, exiting 128 plus the signal number. Apply and removal serialize on an exclusive flock on the data home itself -- the same lock `make install` and `make uninstall` hold for their whole run -- so removal cannot unlink the exposed path while an apply selects it; apply re-checks the installed theme under the lock and keeps it through linking and selection, so an uninstall cannot remove the theme between the check and selection. Removal without an existing data home still works, because apply cannot pass its installed-theme check in that state. A data home that vanishes during apply refuses the lock rather than being created, and a run that cannot take that lock within the bounded wait refuses by name without changing anything. When `make uninstall` afterwards removes the installed icon theme while `kcminputrc` still holds the `[Mouse] cursorTheme=org.macos8.desktop` selection `plasma-apply-cursortheme` wrote, it prints a diagnostic-only warning naming the external reset `plasma-apply-cursortheme breeze_cursors` — `make remove-cursor` owns only the `$HOME/.icons` link so nothing under make may edit `kcminputrc` — reading the file the way `tools/cursor_config.py` reads it (first-line UTF-8 BOM stripped, lines trimmed, section headers switch the section, `#`/`;` comment lines are never keys, first `=` splits key and value with both sides trimmed, a later duplicate's value wins) while leaving every byte of the file identical. | `tests/test_apply_cursor.py::TestInstall`, `tests/test_apply_cursor.py::TestInstall::test_unreadable_cursor_symlink_is_reported_without_traceback`, `tests/test_apply_cursor.py::TestRemove`, `tests/test_apply_cursor.py::TestSelection`, `tests/test_apply_cursor.py::TestSelection::test_sigterm_kills_the_selector_group_and_removes_scratch`, `tests/test_apply_cursor_locks.py::TestDataHomeLock`, `tests/test_cursor_uninstall_diagnostic.py` | No reference screenshot shows the mouse pointer, so no pixel is sampled; the `~/.icons` path, the `org.macos8.desktop` theme name and the `plasma-apply-cursortheme` selector are Xcursor/KDE-required values, and `breeze_cursors` is KDE's default pointer theme taken from the existing removal note. |
| `make apply`'s re-selections run under the data-home flock `make install` and `make uninstall` hold for their whole run: `lookandfeeltool`, `plasma-apply-colorscheme`, `plasma-apply-desktoptheme` and the two `kwriteconfig6` decoration-button writes (plus the best-effort Kvantum selection and KWin reconfigure) all run while this apply holds it, an install or uninstall that holds the lock past the bounded wait is refused with exit 2 naming the data home before any selection runs, the lock is released for the next run, and an environment that resolves no absolute data home is refused the same way, since it is the path `make install` would already have refused. | `tests/test_apply_theme_locks.py::TestDataHomeLock` | none |

| The CI check job runs the suite with `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen`, so the Qt-based apply tools start without the runner's absent display while the render session pins its own clients back to the nested Wayland platform; the install step also provides `plasma-widgets-addons` (verified through the calculator applet the applet render opens) and `libtesseract5` with `tesseract-ocr-eng` data for Spectacle's OCR probe, the job forces software GL (`LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1`) so KWin's virtual backend has an EGL driver without a GPU, and the install selects the release's Aurorae decoration package (`kwin-style-aurorae` on 26.04, `kwin-common` on 24.04). | `tests/test_ci_workflow.py::TestCiWorkflow::test_workflow_runs_the_suite_on_the_headless_qt_platform`, `tests/test_ci_workflow.py::TestCiWorkflow::test_workflow_installs_and_verifies_the_plasma_render_tools`, `tests/test_ci_workflow.py::TestCiWorkflow::test_workflow_skips_package_install_when_render_tools_exist` | The hosted runner has no `DISPLAY` or `XDG_SESSION_TYPE`; the unmodified suite reproduced the Qt xcb abort in `test_colorscheme_install` and `test_desktoptheme` on run 42621380033. The workflow's `MESA_LOADER_DRIVER_OVERRIDE: llvmpipe` line was removed 2026-11-09: the hosted run `18ded7fe` failed at the install step before any test, so the override's runner-side value was never observed, and the local GPU-host finding (override set → `KMS: DRM_IOCTL_MODE_CREATE_DUMB failed: Permission denied` on the render node, the virtual session captures nothing; `LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1` alone → clean session) was verified 2026-11-09 per the BUGS-FIXED.md entry "the render tests' KWin session cannot allocate its capture buffers under the workflow's llvmpipe env". The runner-side virtual session under the remaining env stays unobserved until the next hosted run that reaches the suite. |
| The fifteen dialog render gates and the applet render gate skip when Mesa is forced onto a software driver (`LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1` or `MESA_LOADER_DRIVER_OVERRIDE`/`GALLIUM_DRIVER` naming llvmpipe, softpipe, swrast or zink), because KWin's ScreenShot2 offscreen effect hangs on a software renderer until KWin 6.8's software-buffer fix (`add43708`); a GPU-backed host runs the captures, and the CI job's software-GL forcing (`LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1`) skips them with that reason. | `tests/test_render_dialog_gates.py::TestDialogGateSkipGuards::test_the_skip_guard_also_covers_a_software_renderer`, `tests/test_render_dialog_gates.py::TestDialogGateSkipGuards::test_the_widget_dialog_gates_skip_only_when_a_tool_is_absent` | The hang reproduced locally with `MESA_LOADER_DRIVER_OVERRIDE=llvmpipe` (Spectacle reports `Did not receive a reply` and writes no PNG); KWin 6.8.0's `offscreeneffect.cpp` carries `software = device->isSoftwareDevice()`, and 6.6.6/6.7.0 do not. |

| Environment supplied make variables are read as literal text by the parse-time shell and control-byte guards; embedded GNU make function syntax such as `$(shell ...)` is refused before execution, including by the refusal diagnostics themselves, which echo the raw text (`$(value)`) instead of expanding the guarded variable. | `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafeInstallPaths::test_make_function_text_in_HOME_is_refused_without_evaluation`, `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafePython::test_make_function_text_in_PYTHON_is_refused_without_evaluation`, `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafeInstallPackages::test_make_function_text_in_INSTALL_PACKAGES_is_refused_without_evaluation` | none |
| A `DESTDIR`, `XDG_DATA_HOME`, `HOME`, `FIREFOX_PROFILE`, `FLOCK_TIMEOUT`, `RENDER_OUTPUT`, `COLOR_SCHEME`, `COLOR_SCHEME_NAME`, `COLOR_SCHEME_ID` or `THEME_ROOT` carrying a recipe-shell metacharacter -- a double quote, `$`, a backtick or a backslash, each shell syntax inside the double quotes these variables are expanded into -- is refused at make level before any recipe of any target runs, and the diagnostic names the variable, the offending character and the value; the same guard refuses a value carrying a control byte -- any of 0x01-0x1f or 0x7f except tab and newline: make word-splits the byte list on blank characters, so the VT, FF and CR bytes are refused by explicit byte tests instead -- without echoing the value back, because the guard's own diagnostic and `require_flock_timeout`'s refusal echo print guarded values raw, where a byte such as 0x1b mangles or forges the terminal text; tab and newline stay legal (a tab is inert spacing and a newline only starts a new line, so neither rewrites already-printed terminal text), and a metacharacter beside either is still refused; a space or an apostrophe stays legal, and the scheme id derivation passes the source file's basename to its shell through the environment instead of a quoted shell word, so no value carried in `COLOR_SCHEME` reaches that shell's text. `COLOR_SCHEME_NAME` joins the same guard for its override alone: a command-line definition precedes parse and survives the makefile's own derivation from `COLOR_SCHEME` (an environment value is refused too, before the makefile assignment replaces it), while the derived default is still undefined when the guard runs, so the guard only ever sees a caller's value; the name is expanded into the double-quoted staging/removal recipe words and rides the exported environment the derivation shell reads. `PYTHON` is also refused without echo when it carries a control byte; otherwise its shell syntax at the unquoted position it is expanded into is refused (a value may carry interpreter arguments, so the words cannot be double-quoted): those four plus an apostrophe, a semicolon, a pipe, an ampersand, a parenthesis, a redirection character or a newline, reported as the word `newline`; a space stays legal because the multi-word form is the documented one. `CHECK_PATTERN` joins the double-quoted guard (its value is expanded into `set -- "$(CHECK_PATTERN)"` and `find ... -name "$value"`); its value additionally reaches the single-quoted `-p '$(CHECK_PATTERN)'` recipe word, so an apostrophe — inert inside double quotes — has its own refusal naming that word; a space stays legal. `INSTALL_PACKAGES` is expanded unquoted (`set -- $(INSTALL_PACKAGES)`), so its refusal covers the PYTHON set: the double-quoted four plus an apostrophe, semicolon, pipe, ampersand, parenthesis, redirection character or newline; a space stays legal because the value is a list of family names. `CHECK_TESTS` and `CHECK_PARALLEL` join the same double-quoted refusal: their run-time guards (`require_check_tests`, `require_check_parallel`) paste the value into the double-quoted words `set -- "$(CHECK_TESTS)"` and `set -- "$(CHECK_PARALLEL)"`, so a double quote, `$`, a backtick or a backslash would rewrite those guard lines themselves and run as shell syntax before either guard's own logic can refuse anything — those four are refused at parse time, before any recipe, with a diagnostic naming the variable, offending character and value; the same guard refuses without echoing the value when it carries a control byte (0x01-0x1f or 0x7f except tab and newline, e.g. 0x1b), which would otherwise be pasted raw into the quoted value of the guards' mid-recipe diagnostics, since make does check these two joins before their `?=` defaults are read, the way `FLOCK_TIMEOUT` joined its list early; the guards' own alphabet tests keep refusing the wider punctuation (a semicolon, an apostrophe and the like, inert inside the double quotes) and name the value from the shell-copied `$$1` argument, not the raw value. `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is never expanded into a recipe word by make — the uninstall recipe reads it through the shell and, when a stale selection still names ours, echoes the settings.ini and gtk.css paths under it — so a value carrying a control byte is refused at parse time (shell metacharacters stay legal, sitting inside the recipe shell's double quotes), echoing nothing of the value. | `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafeInstallPaths`, `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafeFirefoxProfile`, `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafeColorScheme`, `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafeColorSchemeName`, `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafeControlCharacters`, `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafePython`, `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafeRenderOutput`, `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafeCheckPattern`, `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafeThemeRoot`, `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafeInstallPackages`, `tests/test_apply_theme.py::TestMakeApply::test_a_hostile_DESTDIR_value_is_refused_without_running_its_shell`, `tests/test_make_refusals.py::TestShellUnsafeCheckVariables`, `tests/test_theme_install.py::TestShellUnsafeXdgConfigHome` | none |

| The external-command supervisor's timeout bounds the whole run: after killing a command's process group, the reap wait is bounded too, so a child the kernel has not released yet does not stall the caller, and a reap timeout never replaces the timeout result or an in-flight `SystemExit`/`KeyboardInterrupt` from the first wait. The shared `process_group.popen_deferred` defers project-managed SIGTERM/SIGHUP and default SIGINT during child startup; on a failed start it restores the handlers and replays the first recorded signal before re-raising, and on a successful start the command and parallel-worker callers restore them and replay the first recorded signal inside their own cleanup scopes, so a signal arriving even as the handlers are swapped back is raised where the group kill runs instead of unwinding before the cleanup exists; custom SIGTERM/SIGHUP handlers retain their behavior. | The default `make check` runs through `tools/check_parallel.py`, which coordinates one unittest worker per discovered module and reports scratch-directory creation failure and an unlistable test directory with its shared exit-2 diagnostic (a subdirectory swept away or permission-locked while discovery walks the tree raises the discovered-error class naming the directory, so one unreadable directory stops the run loudly before any worker starts instead of a bare `OSError` traceback naming neither, and skipping it would silently drop its modules from the run; the coordinator's two own usage refusals -- a `--tests-dir` that names no directory and a pattern that matches no module -- ride the same `check: error:` exit-2 diagnostic, like every other check-stream input error); `TestCoordinator` pins all coordinator behaviors. `tools/check_parallel_discovery.py` mirrors unittest's filename and package recursion rules, skips symlinked directories and symlinked module files to keep discovery inside the selected test tree, returns file modules only, and assigns collision-safe log paths; `TestDiscovery` pins discovery, log naming, and the unlistable-directory error for both a subdirectory and the selected top-level directory. `tools/check_parallel_workers.py` bounds concurrency, starts each worker in its own process group with stdin from DEVNULL via `process_group.popen_deferred`, kills timed-out groups via `process_group.kill_group` and `process_group.reap`, cleans up on termination signals or Ctrl-C, and records a worker whose log cannot be opened as its own failure with one stderr diagnostic instead of unwinding the run. The coordinator also queues its workers by each module's previous run's recorded wall duration when the small JSON durations cache under the XDG cache home holds any (slowest first, so the multi-second real-render suite starts covered by the other workers instead of after a batch of second-scale modules; entries for modules absent from the current tree are dropped when a run saves, absent or equal entries keep discovery (alphabetical) order among themselves, and results still print in discovery order whatever the queue was), records each finished module's elapsed worker time -- including a timed-out module's timeout span -- during the queued run, and atomically rewrites the cache with this run's modules merged over the remaining recorded ones before printing results (a narrower run -- a targeted pattern, or one whose every module never recorded -- keeps the durations a full run left for the modules it did not touch, so a targeted single-module run cannot return the next full queue to alphabetical order, while a module removed or renamed since its last run leaves no entry behind; the whole-tree module list the merge prunes by is a single directory walk, and a whole-tree walk or cache read failing at save time stays inside the same best-effort stderr note); both directions are best-effort, so a missing, malformed or unwritable cache leaves plain discovery order and an unwritable save costs only one stderr note without touching the exit status. The ordering, recording, and cache-steering cases in `tests/test_check_parallel_order.py` pin these behaviors; `TestSpawnDefenses` and the timeout, signal, and unopenable-log cases in `TestCoordinator` pin the rest of these guarantees. `tools/check_parallel_results.py` validates the exit-5 empty-run summary, fails when a worker log cannot be read, and the exit-5 started-test scan opens the worker log through the same nonblocking regular-file reader the bounded tail read uses, so a worker log that is a regular file for the tail read but a FIFO by the scan fails the module as unreadable instead of stalling the result report on an open that waits for a writer, escapes terminal controls in both the verdict header's module name (discovery's filename validator covers a module's final file name but not the package-directory names in its prefix, so a checked-in test directory may carry an ESC byte or a newline into the module name) and the replayed worker log text, closes unterminated logs before the next section, prints results in discovery order, caps each section's printed worker log at a bounded 64 KiB tail (a worker killed while flooding its log cannot load a multi-gigabyte read that MemoryErrors the whole results report; the hidden byte count is named on a leading `check:` note line and unittest's end-of-log summary stays inside the window, so the verdicts are unchanged; a window with no line break at all is shown whole with the byte counts still named, and CRLF/CR worker logs still read as one line per record as the text-mode whole-file read they replaced folded them), and determines the final exit status; the named verdict, ordering, unreadable-log, terminal-control, and flooded-log cases in `TestCoordinator` pin those behaviors. The serial `make check CHECK_PARALLEL=0` path remains available. | `tests/test_check_parallel.py::TestCoordinator`,
