| The public PNG decoders annotate both accepted input types, `bytes` and `bytearray`. | `tests/test_png_read.py::TestDecoderInputAnnotations::test_public_decoders_annotate_their_accepted_bytearray_input` | none |
| PNG chunk types contain four ASCII letters, and their reserved third letter is uppercase. | `tests/test_png_chunks.py::TestChunkFraming::test_rejects_chunk_type_that_is_not_four_ascii_letters`, `tests/test_png_chunks.py::TestChunkFraming::test_rejects_chunk_type_with_lowercase_reserved_bit` | none |
| Reading a PNG's declared size reads only a header-sized prefix instead of the whole file, falls back to a full read when that prefix cannot frame the first chunk so a misordered stream still gets the decoder's diagnosis, and still refuses by name a file over the byte cap whose header is otherwise valid. | `tests/test_png_read.py::TestReadPng::test_read_png_size_reads_only_the_header_prefix`, `tests/test_png_read.py::TestReadPng::test_read_png_size_falls_back_when_the_prefix_cuts_off_the_first_chunk`, `tests/test_png_read.py::TestReadPng::test_read_png_size_still_rejects_an_oversize_file` | none |
| A PNG chunk stream carrying a second IHDR, PLTE or tRNS chunk is refused by name instead of letting the later chunk silently redefine the image. | `tests/test_png_chunks.py::TestChunkFraming` | none |
| A PNG chunk stream whose IDAT chunks are not consecutive, or whose PLTE or tRNS chunk follows the image data, is refused by name rather than decoded as if its chunk order were valid. | `tests/test_png_chunks.py::TestChunkFraming` | none |
| A PNG chunk stream carrying a complete chunk, or a shorter trailing fragment that cannot form one, after IEND is refused by name instead of decoded while silently dropping those bytes, because IEND must be the last chunk. | `tests/test_png_chunks.py::TestChunkFraming` | none |
| A PNG chunk stream whose IEND chunk carries a payload is refused by name instead of its non-empty data field being silently dropped, because IEND's data field must be empty. | `tests/test_png_chunks.py::TestChunkFraming` | none |
| A PNG's `tRNS` chunk is refused unless the header's color type and palette can carry it — never for the alpha color types 4 and 6, only at its exact length for the greyscale and truecolor sample types, and only for 1 to the palette's entry count for color type 3 — on both the RGB and RGBA decode paths. | `tests/test_png_malformed.py::TestTrnsColorTypeValidation` | none |
| A PNG's `tRNS` chunk that precedes a `PLTE` chunk which is present is refused on both decode paths for every color type — the palette image's required palette and a truecolor image's suggested palette alike — while a color type 3 image with no `PLTE` at all keeps its missing-palette diagnosis. | `tests/test_png_malformed.py::TestTrnsColorTypeValidation` | none |
| A PNG's `PLTE` chunk is refused for the greyscale color types 0 and 4, where the PNG spec prohibits it, while a suggested palette on the truecolor color types 2 and 6 and the required palette on color type 3 still decode, on both the RGB and RGBA decode paths. | `tests/test_png_malformed.py::TestPlteColorTypeValidation` | none |
| The PNG decoder refuses bytes after the end of the IDAT zlib stream instead of silently dropping them, so the concatenated IDAT payload holds exactly one zlib stream. | `tests/test_png_malformed.py::TestMalformedImageData::test_rejects_trailing_bytes_after_the_zlib_stream` | none |
| The cursor generator's nearest-neighbour hotspot mapping keeps every frame's pinned hotspot inside that frame: it rounds the grid hotspot up to the first destination pixel its source pixel covers, clamped to the last rendered pixel (size - 1), which bounds it even at a nominal size below the 16x16 grid where the downscale skips source pixels. This covers the grid entries only: the vector arrow, I-beam and pointing hand are rasterized at each size, not scaled, and their hotspots are design-unit points checked against the design grid (a vertex or a point strictly inside the face). It also refuses a nominal size above the Xcursor frame-dimension limit (0x7FFF) before allocating a pixel buffer, in both the generator's size list and a direct render, naming the limit instead of failing later in the writer. | `tests/test_make_cursors.py::TestGeneratorValidation::test_a_size_below_the_grid_keeps_its_hotspot_inside_the_frame`, `tests/test_make_cursors.py::TestGeneratorValidation::test_a_size_below_the_grid_maps_each_hotspot_to_its_first_covered_pixel`, `tests/test_make_cursors.py::TestGeneratorValidation::test_a_nominal_size_above_the_xcursor_frame_limit_is_refused`, `tests/test_make_cursors.py::TestGeneratorValidation::test_render_rgba_refuses_a_size_above_the_xcursor_frame_limit` | none |
| Both Xcursor writers refuse an empty frame list, naming that at least one frame is required, instead of writing a zero-TOC file with no image to draw. | `tests/test_xcursor.py::TestWriterRejections::test_rejects_an_empty_image_list` | none |
| The antialiased cursor rasterizer (`tools/cursor_vector.py`) draws a `VectorCursor` polygon face with a white outline band as straight RGBA: each pixel takes 4x4 sub-samples, a sample inside the face under the even-odd rule is black and one outside it within `outline_width` of an edge is white, and the alpha and grey are integer round-half-up shares of the covered and white samples, so a fully clear pixel stays `(0, 0, 0, 0)`; `vector_hotspot` maps a design-unit hotspot in `[0, design_size)` to a pixel inside the frame. | `tests/test_cursor_vector.py::TestRenderFace::test_half_covered_edge_pixel_has_half_alpha` | none |
| The test-side selector matcher decodes a backslash escape in an attribute selector's quoted or unquoted value, so an escaped quote or backslash, a CSS string continuation, and a hex escape — whose one-whitespace terminator belongs to the escape, not the value — test the character or string they denote instead of being read as the string's end. | `tests/test_selector_matching.py::TestSelectorMatching::test_an_escaped_quote_inside_an_attribute_value_is_literal` `tests/test_selector_matching.py::TestSelectorMatching::test_a_backslash_newline_continues_a_quoted_css_string` `tests/test_selector_matching.py::TestSelectorMatching::test_a_hex_escape_with_its_whitespace_lives_in_an_unquoted_value` | none |
| The two test-side unchanged-file caches (the SVG tree cache and the Xcursor parse cache) key their entries on one shared stat fingerprint: it is the documented `(st_ino, st_size, st_mtime_ns, st_ctime_ns)` identity, differs between files and after a rewrite, stays equal for an untouched file, and a file that cannot be statted yields no identity. | `tests/test_file_identity.py::TestFingerprintOrNone` | none |
| The suite's own Python process imports no GTK introspection: the GTK 3 and GTK 4 pixel suites run their GTK work in child interpreters, so GLib's C worker threads (invisible to `threading.active_count()`) never sit in the process that runs the `os.fork` FIFO guards, which Python 3.12+ deprecates for a multi-threaded parent as a deadlock hazard. | `tests/test_no_gtk_introspection.py::TestNoGtkIntrospection` | none |
| The installers' shared bounded-wait flock retries an interrupted `flock` call instead of refusing, acquires after a holder releases within the wait, and releases the descriptor even when the lock body raises, so a signal or a failing install body cannot leave the config home, data home or profile locked for later runs. | `tests/test_file_lock.py::TestExclusiveFlock` `tests/test_file_lock.py::TestInstalledDataLock` | none |
| Repeated interrupted `flock` calls check the shared installer lock's deadline before retrying, and a timeout closes the descriptor without entering the protected body (recorded 2026-10-29 by the improve role). | `tests/test_file_lock.py::TestExclusiveFlock::test_repeated_interruptions_still_honor_the_deadline` | none |

| Parallel check worker logs are opened as regular files without blocking on a FIFO or device swapped into a scratch log path, both when spawning workers and when printing results; an invalid path fails that module and reporting continues. | `tests/test_check_parallel.py::TestCoordinator::test_fifo_worker_log_fails_without_blocking`, `tests/test_check_parallel.py::TestCoordinator::test_fifo_worker_log_read_fails_without_blocking` | Worker timeout cannot supervise a blocking filesystem open, so descriptors use `O_NONBLOCK` and are checked with `fstat` before use. |
| The reference-pixel sampler's `--histogram` counts RGB pixels, or RGBA tuples with `--alpha`, only within the requested region; ties sort by ascending tuple and text/JSON preserve RGB beneath transparency with opaque fallback. | `tests/test_sample_histogram.py::TestSampleHistogram` (2026-11-02) | Histogram output added 2026-11-01; alpha-aware counts added 2026-11-02 by the feature role. |
| The sampler's optional `--top-colors` selects the requested highest-count RGB or RGBA colors with tuple tie ordering while reporting distinct-color count and omitted region pixels; invalid use is rejected before image reads and decoding remains row-bounded. | `tests/test_sample_histogram.py::TestSampleHistogram` (2026-11-02) | Counts cover the full validated region; with a limit, selection avoids sorting the full distinct-color set. Alpha-aware counting added 2026-11-02 by the feature role. |
| The sampler's optional `--color-offset` pages ranked histogram colors after all filters, bounds selection storage by original distinct-color count, and reports offset metadata only when explicitly used. | `tests/test_sample_histogram_offset.py`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` | Ranking is descending count then ascending color tuple; page fields describe emitted colors (added 2026-11-03 by the feature role). |
| The sampler's optional `--min-color-count` filters histogram colors inclusively before `--top-colors`, reports original distinct-color totals and all omitted region pixels, and applies spatial diagnostics only to emitted colors. | `tests/test_sample_histogram_min_count.py::TestSampleHistogramMinimumCount`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` | Fractions keep the full crop denominator; invalid option use is rejected before image reads (added 2026-11-02 by the feature role), and a minimum above its paired maximum is refused before image reads (added 2026-11-11 by the improve role). |
| The sampler's optional `--min-color-fraction` filters histogram colors by count divided by the full region area before `--top-colors`; it combines with the minimum-count filter, reports original distinct-color totals and omitted pixels, and applies spatial diagnostics only to emitted colors. | `tests/test_sample_histogram_min_fraction.py::TestSampleHistogramMinimumFraction`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` | Uses the same Python float division as `--color-fractions`; invalid values and modes are rejected before image reads (added 2026-11-02 by the feature role). |
| The sampler's optional `--max-color-fraction` filters histogram colors inclusively by count divided by full region area before `--top-colors`; it intersects with fraction, count and named-color filters, reports original distinct-color totals and omitted pixels, and applies spatial diagnostics only to emitted colors. | `tests/test_sample_histogram_max_fraction.py::TestSampleHistogramMaximumFraction`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` | RGB and RGBA groups use the same full-region denominator; invalid option use is rejected before image reads (added 2026-11-03 by the feature role), and a minimum above its paired maximum is refused before image reads (added 2026-11-11 by the improve role). |
| The sampler's repeatable `--exclude-color` removes named RGB tuples before minimum-count, minimum-fraction and top-color selection, while preserving original distinct-color totals, full-region fractions and emitted-color spatial diagnostics; with `--alpha`, it excludes every opacity variant. | `tests/test_sample_histogram_exclusions.py::TestSampleHistogramExclusions` | RGB syntax uses the shared `rgb_argument`; explicit exclusion reports totals even when the named color is absent (added 2026-11-02 by the feature role). |
| The sampler's repeatable `--include-color` restricts histogram candidates to named RGB tuples before exclusions, count/fraction filters and top-color pagination; it preserves original distinct-color totals and full-region fractions, and with `--alpha` includes each opacity variant. | `tests/test_sample_histogram_inclusions.py::TestSampleHistogramInclusions`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` | RGB syntax uses the shared `rgb_argument`; explicit inclusion reports totals even when it matches no colors or the full palette (added 2026-11-03 by the feature role). |
| The sampler's optional `--color-bounds` reports absolute smallest enclosing bounds for emitted histogram colors, grouping by RGB or exact RGBA with `--alpha`; top-color limits bound only emitted keys and the extra traversal is skipped otherwise. | `tests/test_sample_histogram_bounds.py::TestSampleHistogramBounds` | Bounds enclose disconnected occurrences and do not imply a solid rectangle; alpha uses PNG data with opaque fallback (added 2026-11-02 by the feature role). |
| The reference-pixel sampler's `--find-color` counts exact RGB matches by default, or matches within the explicit per-channel RGB `--color-tolerance` (0–255), within the validated region; optional `--match-alpha` requires an exact alpha byte while preserving RGB beneath transparency, and reports the value in text or JSON. It reports the absolute smallest enclosing bounds or null when absent. Optional `--match-pixels` retains a bounded row-major page of absolute matching coordinates; `--match-pixel-offset` skips that many predicate matches, and count, bounds and optional diagnostics cover the full region while omitted count includes skipped and trailing matches. Invalid option combinations are validated before containment checks or image reads. | `tests/test_sample_find_color.py::TestSampleFindColor`, `tests/test_sample_match_alpha.py::TestSampleMatchAlpha`, `tests/test_sample_match_pixels.py::TestSampleMatchPixels`, `tests/test_sample_match_pixel_offset.py::TestSampleMatchPixelOffset`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation`, `tests/test_sample_validators.py::TestSampleValidators` | `tools/sample_arguments.py::option_error` returns the first compatibility diagnostic without output, mutation or filesystem access; scalar parsers live in `tools/sample_validators.py` and remain re-exported by `tools/sample_arguments.py`, while mutually exclusive modes remain argparse responsibilities. `_search_match` in `tools/sample_report_search.py` owns traversal and match-result assembly; `report_search` owns text/JSON presentation. Alpha filtering uses PNG alpha with opaque fallback and is not a theme visual value; the coordinate list stores no more than the requested limit. |
| The reference-pixel sampler's `--runs` groups adjacent equal RGB pixels, or equal RGBA pixels with `--alpha`, along one validated row or column, or each row/column of a rectangle selected by `--run-axis`; scanline boundaries remain separate and output follows top-to-bottom rows or left-to-right columns. `--min-run-length` and `--max-run-length` filter completed runs inclusively without joining separated runs; repeatable `--run-color` selects and `--exclude-run-color` removes runs by RGB after segmentation, ignoring opacity while retaining separate run records; exclusions win when a color appears in both lists. Missing alpha is opaque and RGB beneath transparency is preserved. | `tests/test_sample_runs.py::TestSampleRuns`, `tests/test_sample_runs_min_length.py::TestSampleRunsMinimumLength`, `tests/test_sample_runs_max_length.py::TestSampleRunsMaximumLength`, `tests/test_sample_runs_colors.py::TestSampleRunsColors`, `tests/test_sample_runs_exclusions.py::TestSampleRunsExclusions`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` | Handler lives in `tools/sample_report_runs.py`; RGBA runs use PNG alpha data without compositing. Rectangular scans and the minimum-length output filter added 2026-11-02; maximum-length and named RGB selection coverage added 2026-11-03 by the feature role; named RGB exclusion added 2026-11-03 by the feature role; a minimum above its paired maximum is refused before image reads (added 2026-11-11 by the improve role). |
| The reference-pixel sampler's optional `--run-count` adds a scalar scanline run total to a `--runs` report: a top-level `run_count` in JSON and a `run count: N` text line immediately after the `axis:` line (after the `longest run:` line when `--longest-run` is also given), counted over every scanline of the region before color selection, exclusion and the inclusive length filters, leaving the listed runs and all other fields unchanged; omitted the flag is absent in key, line and counting, and it requires `--runs`. | `tests/test_sample_run_count.py::TestSampleRunCount`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` (2026-11-03) | Tooling output format added 2026-11-03 by the feature role; no reference pixel provenance applies. |
| The reference-pixel sampler's optional `--scanline-run-counts` adds one run count per scanline to a `--runs` report: a top-level `scanline_run_counts` list of integers in JSON and a `scanline run counts: N, N, ...` text line immediately after the optional `run count` line and before the run list, counting each scanline's runs before color selection, exclusion and the inclusive length filters in scanning order (top-to-bottom rows, left-to-right columns), leaving the listed runs and all other fields unchanged; omitted the flag is absent in key, line and counting, and it requires `--runs`. | `tests/test_sample_scanline_run_counts.py::TestSampleScanlineRunCounts`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` (2026-11-03) | Tooling output format added 2026-11-03 by the feature role; no reference pixel provenance applies. |
| The reference-pixel sampler's optional `--longest-run` adds the dominant scanline run of a `--runs` report as a scalar: a top-level `longest_run` in JSON mirroring one emitted `runs` record (`x`, `y`, `length`, `rgb`, `hex`, plus `alpha` under `--alpha`) and a `longest run: X,Y,LENGTH` text line immediately after the `axis:` line, taken over every scan run of the region before color selection, exclusion and the inclusive length filters with ties resolved in scanning order (top-to-bottom rows, left-to-right columns, earliest run within a scanline); the listed runs and all other fields stay unchanged, omitted the flag is absent in key, line and comparison, and it requires `--runs`. | `tests/test_sample_longest_run.py::TestSampleLongestRun`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` (2026-11-03) | Tooling output format added 2026-11-03 by the feature role; no reference pixel provenance applies. |
| The reference-pixel sampler's `--alpha` includes the PNG alpha byte in ordinary pixel and rectangle output, preserving RGB and using 255 for opaque images; the decode remains bounded to the requested bottom row. | `tests/test_sample_alpha.py::TestSampleAlpha` | Alpha is PNG transparency data, not a theme visual value. | The sampler's optional `--match-rows` reports dense per-row counts for the full requested region using the color-search RGB tolerance and exact-alpha predicate; zero-match rows are included, coordinate limits do not truncate counts, and output is omitted by default. | `tests/test_sample_match_rows.py::TestSampleMatchRows` | Row indices are relative to the region's first row; collection shares the existing positioned traversal (added 2026-11-02 by the feature role). |
| The sampler's optional `--match-columns` reports dense per-column counts for the full requested region using the color-search RGB tolerance and exact-alpha predicate; zero-match columns are included, coordinate limits do not truncate counts, and output is omitted by default. | `tests/test_sample_match_columns.py::TestSampleMatchColumns` | Column indices are relative to the region's first column; collection shares the existing positioned traversal (added 2026-11-02 by the feature role). |
| The sampler's optional `--match-row-bounds` reports the smallest absolute horizontal interval enclosing matches on each requested row using the color-search RGB tolerance and exact-alpha predicate; empty rows are null, disconnected matches include gaps, and coordinate limits do not truncate the spans. | `tests/test_sample_match_row_bounds.py::TestSampleMatchRowBounds` | Dense row indices are relative to the crop and spans share the positioned traversal (added 2026-11-02 by the feature role). |
| The sampler's optional `--match-column-bounds` reports the smallest absolute vertical interval enclosing matches in each requested column using the color-search RGB tolerance and exact-alpha predicate; empty columns are null, disconnected matches include gaps, and coordinate limits do not truncate the spans. | `tests/test_sample_match_column_bounds.py::TestSampleMatchColumnBounds` | Dense column indices are relative to the crop and spans share the positioned traversal (added 2026-11-02 by the feature role). |
| The sampler's optional `--longest-match-run` reports the longest contiguous horizontal match interval in absolute coordinates, retains the earliest row-major interval on ties, uses the color-search tolerance and exact-alpha predicate, and summarizes the full region without retaining the run list unless requested separately. | `tests/test_sample_longest_match_run.py::TestSampleLongestMatchRun` | Summary collection shares the positioned traversal and is omitted by default (added 2026-11-02 by the feature role). |
| The sampler's optional `--longest-match-column-run` reports the longest contiguous vertical match interval in absolute coordinates, preferring greater length then ascending `(x, y)`, uses the color-search tolerance and exact-alpha predicate, and summarizes the full region without retaining the column-run list unless requested separately. | `tests/test_sample_longest_match_column_run.py::TestSampleLongestMatchColumnRun` | Summary collection shares the positioned traversal and is omitted by default (added 2026-11-02 by the feature role). |
| The sampler's optional minimum and maximum horizontal match-run lengths filter emitted runs by inclusive length; optional `--max-match-runs` reports up to N eligible intervals in row-major order, with `--match-run-offset` skipping eligible intervals globally after filtering. The omitted count includes skipped and trailing eligible intervals. Full match statistics and longest-run summaries still cover the region, intervals never merge across gaps or row boundaries, and capped collection retains at most N horizontal intervals. | `tests/test_sample_match_runs_min_length.py::TestSampleMatchRunsMinLength`, `tests/test_sample_match_runs_max_length.py::TestSampleMatchRunsMaxLength`, `tests/test_sample_match_runs_limit.py::TestSampleMatchRunsLimit`, `tests/test_sample_match_runs_offset.py::TestSampleMatchRunsOffset`, `tests/test_sample_match_runs_storage.py`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` | Length filtering, page offset and cap are applied as intervals close; the collector keeps the eligible total separately from retained page records (added 2026-11-09 by the feature role), and a minimum above its paired maximum is refused before image reads (added 2026-11-11 by the improve role). |
| The sampler's optional minimum and maximum vertical match-run lengths filter emitted column runs by inclusive length; optional `--max-match-column-runs` reports up to N eligible intervals in column-major order, with `--match-column-run-offset` skipping eligible intervals globally after filtering. The omitted count includes skipped and trailing eligible intervals. Match statistics and longest-run summaries still cover the region, intervals never merge across gaps or column boundaries, and when the cap is set a second column-major predicate scan over the already-decoded region collects the page, so capped collection retains at most N vertical intervals. | `tests/test_sample_match_column_runs_min_length.py::TestSampleMatchColumnRunsMinLength`, `tests/test_sample_match_column_runs_max_length.py::TestSampleMatchColumnRunsMaxLength`, `tests/test_sample_match_column_runs_limit.py::TestSampleMatchColumnRunsLimit`, `tests/test_sample_match_column_runs_offset.py::TestSampleMatchColumnRunsOffset`, `tests/test_sample_match_column_runs_storage.py`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` | Length filtering, page offset and cap are applied as intervals close during the capped column-major scan; the collector keeps the eligible total separately from retained page records, invalid offset use is rejected before image reads, and a minimum above its paired maximum is refused before image reads (maximum added 2026-11-03; cap added 2026-11-04; paging added 2026-11-04; capped storage bound added 2026-11-09 by the feature role; refusal added 2026-11-11 by the improve role). |
| When GTK staging fails, every earlier staging file is discarded; if a staging file cannot be removed, the reported `ConfigError` names the failed cleanup path as well as the original staging failure. | `tests/test_gtk_staging.py::TestStageAll::test_a_failed_later_write_discards_the_earlier_staging`, `tests/test_gtk_staging.py::TestStageAll::test_reports_when_failed_staging_cannot_be_discarded` | Ordinary cleanup failures are reported; recovery after SIGKILL remains outside this guarantee. Added 2026-11-02 by the robustness role. |
| The sampler's optional `--row-color-counts` reports one distinct-color count per crop row over the complete requested region in top-to-bottom order, grouped like emitted colors (RGB alone, RGBA with `--alpha` including each transparent pixel's stored RGB), unaffected by displayed-palette filters and paging, and omitted by default. | `tests/test_sample_histogram_row_counts.py::TestSampleHistogramRowCounts`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` | Counting uses one positioned traversal of the full region (added 2026-11-03 by the feature role). |
| The sampler's optional `--column-color-counts` reports one distinct-color count per crop column over the complete requested region in left-to-right order, grouped like emitted colors (RGB alone, RGBA with `--alpha` including each transparent pixel's stored RGB), unaffected by displayed-palette filters and paging, and omitted by default. | `tests/test_sample_histogram_column_counts.py::TestSampleHistogramColumnCounts`, `tests/test_sample_option_validation.py::TestSampleOptionValidation` | Counting uses one positioned traversal of the full region (added 2026-11-03 by the feature role). |
| The parallel check trusts an exit-5 `NO TESTS RAN` summary only when no unittest verbose test-start marker appears anywhere in that worker's log; this scan stays bounded in memory even when printed output is truncated to its tail. | `tests/test_check_parallel.py::TestCoordinator::test_a_forged_no_tests_ran_summary_fails_the_run`, `tests/test_check_parallel.py::TestCoordinator::test_a_started_test_hidden_before_the_bounded_log_tail_fails` | The marker scan reads fixed-size chunks while the displayed log remains a bounded tail. |

The dialog's client is not `kdialog` itself but the helper daemon `kdialog_progress_helper` that kdialog execs for a progress dialog: measured on this host (kdialog 25.04.0, 2026-11-10), the `kdialog --progressbar` launcher prints the D-Bus reference and exits within the first second, so the shared session script's liveness check would fail the render after the settle while the dialog outlives its killed launcher; the helper runs the same dialog itself and survives the settle, and the missing-tool check still names `kdialog`, which ships it. The meter never moves -- only its D-Bus interface drives it, and the render session calls none of it -- so no striped `progress-pattern` tile and no busy animation enters any crop. (added 2026-11-10 by the feature role) |

| The calendar dialog pins the rendered palette-painted day table the `kdialog --calendar` client opens: the flat #DDDDDD window face ([Colors:Window] `BackgroundNormal`) above the day table with no other ink inside, the selected day's #CCCCFF fill ([Colors:Selection] `BackgroundNormal`) with the selected day's hinted black digit ink and the white day-table face ([Colors:View] `BackgroundNormal`) on both sides, the weekday-letter row's hinted ink cores over the white face (the leading columns' cores at [Colors:View] `ForegroundInactive` #888888 and the trailing columns' at full-strength black -- a mixed-ink measured blend the reference set cannot ground, recorded rather than widened), and the leftmost day column's red Sunday digit cores (the measured #800000 ink is darker than its nearest palette entry, [Colors:View] `ForegroundNegative` 204,0,0 -- the measured blend is the contract and the discrepancy is recorded instead of widening the band). The day grid is caller-locale-independent: the scratch session pins the measurement locale (en_US.UTF-8) over the caller's LANG and LC_* and drops LANGUAGE, so kdialog's calendar starts its grid on Sunday whatever the invoking shell exports, and a session that leaks the caller's locale shifts the grid one column left and fails the red Sunday-ink pins. | `tests/test_render_dialog_gates.py::TestCalendarDialogRender`, `tests/test_render_dialog_gates.py::TestCalendarLocaleDialogRender` | No collected reference screenshot shows a dialog calendar, so the comparison values are the colorscheme palette's own entries and the measured geometry, not a reference crop. The client pins `--default 1970-01-07` with `--dateformat yyyy-MM-dd`: probed on this host (kdialog 25.04.0, 2026-11-10), without `--dateformat` kdialog ignores `--default` and opens today's month, while with it the default applies in either option order -- so the view never contains today's date and no today marker, clock-driven selection or month-by-month layout drift can enter any crop. The crops were measured on two consecutive full `tools/render_surface.py --calendar-dialog` runs on this host on 2026-11-10 whose two 480x360 captures agreed byte for byte under `cmp`. The pins describe that en_US-default caller's Sunday-first day grid, so the render session pins the measurement locale (`tools/render_session.py::MEASUREMENT_LOCALE`, en_US.UTF-8, over the caller's `LANG`/`LC_*`, with `LANGUAGE` dropped): a caller's Monday-first locale (and Qt's C locale, which an unset LANG gives) shifts the whole grid one column left and turns the red Sunday-ink pins dark, gray and white -- reproduced 2026-11-12 by the bugfix role with a `--calendar-dialog` render under `LANG=de_DE.UTF-8` (a locale name the host's glibc does not even have generated; Qt derives the locale from the string alone), whose pinned points measured exactly the recorded failure's values, and with the same render under an entirely unset locale. No collected reference screenshot shows a dialog calendar, so the measurement locale's source is the caller default the measured pin runs ran under, not a reference surface. `TestCalendarLocaleDialogRender` renders the calendar under a hostile Monday-first caller (`tests/support/render_gates.py::Renders.HOSTILE_ENVIRONMENTS`) so a session that leaks the caller's locale again fails there instead of on a user's shell. (added 2026-11-10 by the feature role; locale pin added 2026-11-12 by the bugfix role) |

The bands were measured on two consecutive full `tools/render_surface.py --color-dialog` runs on this host (kdialog 25.04.0, 2026-11-10) whose two 480x360 captures agreed byte for byte under `cmp`; the bands sit in flat one-color rows clear of the dialog's gradient selector's dithered rows and of every glyph row, and the dialog shows no clock-dependent content -- no date and no clock-driven selection can enter any band -- so the capture needs no date or default pin. | An insensitive GTK 3 view's text paints the existing insensitive grey and its field stays the existing white: the shipped sheet's dated block directly after the existing `label:backdrop selection:disabled, …` disabled-selected group and before the tab-close comment restates the toolkit's three disabled view arms — the byte-equal five-member comma group `.view:disabled, iconview:disabled, .view text:disabled, iconview text:disabled, textview text:disabled` with exactly `color: #888888` and `background-color: #FFFFFF`, and `treeview.view:disabled` and `treeview.view:disabled:backdrop` each with exactly `color: #888888` — pinned by `tests/test_gtk_theme.py::TestGtkTheme::test_disabled_view_face_and_ink` (count-one, whole-dict and token-sweep pins plus the comment-stripped order pin after the existing disabled-selected groups) and by `tests/test_gtk_theme_controls.py::TestGtkThemeControls::test_disabled_view_renders_the_white_field_and_grey_ink` (an insensitive `Gtk.TextView` in a fresh offscreen window reads `#FFFFFF` at its four allocation corners, no allocation pixel reads a stock disabled literal, and one allocation row carries a run of eight or more exact `#888888` pixels; the buffer's underscores are scaled 3x with a `TextTag` because the default-font underscore stroke is a sub-pixel hairline whose pixels never reach full ink coverage). | `tests/test_gtk_theme.py::TestGtkTheme::test_disabled_view_face_and_ink`, `tests/test_gtk_theme_controls.py::TestGtkThemeControls::test_disabled_view_renders_the_white_field_and_grey_ink` | No collected reference screenshot in `macos8.6-screenshots/` shows a disabled GTK view (the standing GTK-scope adaptation premise). The three stock arms were probed 2026-11-11 with `gresource extract /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libgtk-3.so.0 /org/gtk/libgtk/theme/Adwaita/gtk-contained.css` (libgtk-3 3.24.49, the 2055-line resource): line 28 the five-member comma group `color: #929595; background-color: #faf9f8;`, line 699 `treeview.view:disabled { color: #929595; }`, line 705 `treeview.view:disabled:backdrop { color: #d4cfca; }` — the only disabled view arms anywhere in the resource; the dark-twin resource `gtk-contained-dark.css` carries the same-shaped family (comma group `color: #919190; background-color: #323232;` at its line 28, `treeview.view:disabled { color: #919190; }` at line 691, `treeview.view:disabled:backdrop { color: #5b5b5b; }` at line 697). Value sources: the ink is the existing insensitive grey `#888888` re-derived from the colour scheme's `[Colors:Button] ForegroundInactive` by the disabled-text assertion, the same grey the existing disabled label, entry and disabled-selected arms paint; the field reuses the existing white view field the base `.view, textview text, list` group states; the backdrop tree twin keeps that grey in unfocused windows, the policy the existing `row:backdrop:disabled:selected` and `treeview.view:disabled:selected:backdrop` restatements follow. Render-probed 2026-11-11 the same day: the user sheet installs above the toolkit resource (the child probes load it at `GTK_STYLE_PROVIDER_PRIORITY_USER` (800) over the theme resource), so the existing base group already wins per-property — pre-change an insensitive text view or icon view painted the existing white field under the base group's unconditional black ink, never the stock literals; the restatements' observable effect is that ink, which becomes the existing insensitive grey like every other existing insensitive text (mutation: dropping the comma group's `color` declaration renders the base group's black ink and fails the probe's grey-run assertion), while the white-field restatement is a coherence restate whose removal stays unobservable through the base group. The `:disabled:selected` combinations stay owned by the existing disabled-selected groups (including `treeview.view:disabled:selected` and its backdrop twin); the toolkit's remaining insensitive arms outside the view family (label, entry and button fields) are the existing disabled-text records' own; and the GTK 4 sibling — the integration library's per-family disabled view ink arms and the existing 2026-11-09 GTK 4 disabled-view group's deliberately excluded `color` co-declaration — is a separate candidate, not this record. Declaration, count and order checks alone do not establish rendered pixels; the render probe establishes them. (added 2026-11-11 by the feature role) |

Exclusions with reasons: stock's geometry (the line-808 minimums and padding, the lines-810/812 margins) and the lines-810/812 edge hairlines stay inherited per-property as platform scroll geometry and kept toolkit ink — in the disabled state the restated `border-color` wins the equal-specificity tie over the stock line-818 transparent border colour, while the hairline's width and style stay stock; stock's glyph claims are already beaten by the existing glyph groups' asset URLs, which stay mid-sheet; stock declares no checked, active or drop arm for menu arrows. The GTK 4 sibling needs nothing: its sheet's header comment already records that GTK 4 scrolls menus inside a popover through no arrow node. Declaration, count and source-order checks do not establish rendered pixels. (added 2026-11-11 by the feature role) |

| The fidelity CLI reads a JPEG candidate or reference screenshot directly: `tools/fidelity_sources.py::read_source` checks repository containment before any read, then sniffs the input's first bytes and routes a JPEG through the sampler's existing convert bridge (`tools/jpeg_convert.py::convert_jpeg`) into a private temporary PNG it decodes exactly like any PNG input with the same bounded crop policy, deriving an all-`255` alpha plane from the RGB-only decode so the JPEG compares coherently against an RGBA render with no alpha refusal; the temporary directory is private to the call and dropped when it returns; PNG inputs never enter the conversion path; a non-PNG non-JPEG head keeps the standing `not a PNG file` decode refusal; and every JPEG-branch failure names the paths the operator gave, never the temporary decode (`convert_jpeg`'s own log scrub plus the loader's rewrite of the temporary path in any decode failure). | `tests/test_fidelity_jpeg.py` | ImageMagick's `convert` performs the JPEG decode (provenance: the sampler's `convert jpeg:- PNG24:<temp>` bridge reused unchanged; `convert` absent from `PATH` is refused with a diagnostic naming it), no reference screenshot pins the decoder itself, and no lossy pixel value is pinned — the fixture's JPEG self-comparison must report zero differences on any `convert` build, since identical inputs decode identically. (added 2026-11-11 by the feature role) |
